It's actually the opposite—my mind just feels completely blank and blocked off from the event.\n\nDoctor: How have these feelings of disconnection and memory gaps been affecting your daily life and ability to function since the event occurred?\n\nPatient: It has been very difficult to focus or get through my normal daily routine because of these strange feelings and memory gaps. It is hard to get anything done when I feel so disconnected from everything. It's exhausting, even though it has only been going on for about a week now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe in more detail what you mean by feeling 'completely detached' and like an 'outside observer' to your own body and the world around you?\n\nPatient: It feels like I'm hovering outside my own body, watching myself go through the motions as if my physical self doesn't actually belong to me. At the same time, the world around me feels completely foggy and artificial, almost like I'm separated from my surroundings by a thick pane of glass.\n\nDoctor: Since Acute Stress Disorder typically lasts up to a month and Posttraumatic Stress Disorder is considered if symptoms last longer than a month, do you think your symptoms might persist beyond the next few weeks, or do you feel they are already improving or will improve soon?"

It has only been about a week since the traumatic event, so everything is still very fresh and overwhelming. Because it is so recent, I'm really not sure whether these symptoms will persist or if they might start to improve soon. I'm just feeling very lost right now and hoping they don't last.

I'm just feeling very lost right now and hoping they don't last.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the traumatic event that triggered these feelings, and how you think it has been affecting you, considering your current symptoms and emotional state?"
  }

All I really recall is that during the event, time felt like it completely stopped or warped in a very strange way. It's actually the opposite—my mind just feels completely blank and blocked off from the event.\n\nDoctor: How have these feelings of disconnection and memory gaps been affecting your daily life and ability to function since the event occurred?\n\nPatient: It has been very difficult to focus or get through my normal daily routine because of these strange feelings and memory gaps. It is hard to get anything done when I feel so disconnected from everything. It's exhausting, even though it has only been going on for about a week now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe in more detail what you mean by feeling 'completely detached' and like an 'outside observer' to your own body and the world around you?\n\nPatient: It feels like I'm hovering outside my own body, watching myself go through the motions as if my physical self doesn't actually belong to me. At the same time, the world around me feels completely foggy and artificial, almost like I'm separated from my surroundings by a thick pane of glass.\n\nDoctor: Since Acute Stress Disorder typically lasts up to a month and Posttraumatic Stress Disorder is considered if symptoms last longer than a month, do you think your symptoms might persist beyond the next few weeks, or do you feel they are already improving or will improve soon?\n\nPatient: It has only been about a week since the traumatic event, so everything is still very fresh and overwhelming. Because it is so recent, I'm really not sure whether these symptoms will persist or if they might start to improve soon. I'm just feeling very lost right now and hoping they don't last.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the traumatic event that triggered these feelings, and how you think it has been affecting you, considering your current symptoms and emotional state?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I can't tell you much about the event itself because my memory of it is so fragmented and mostly blank. At the same time, the world around me feels completely foggy and artificial, almost like I'm separated from my surroundings by a thick pane of glass.\n\nDoctor: Since Acute Stress Disorder typically lasts up to a month and Posttraumatic Stress Disorder is considered if symptoms last longer than a month, do you think your symptoms might persist beyond the next few weeks, or do you feel they are already improving or will improve soon?\n\nPatient: It has only been about a week since the traumatic event, so everything is still very fresh and overwhelming. Because it is so recent, I'm really not sure whether these symptoms will persist or if they might start to improve soon. I'm just feeling very lost right now and hoping they don't last.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the traumatic event that triggered these feelings, and how you think it has been affecting you, considering your current symptoms and emotional state?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I can't tell you much about the event itself because my memory of it is so fragmented and mostly blank. It's exhausting, even though it has only been going on for about a week now.\n\nDoctor: Can you describe in more detail what you mean by feeling 'completely detached' and like an 'outside observer' to your own body and the world around you?\n\nPatient: It feels like I'm hovering outside my own body, watching myself go through the motions as if my physical self doesn't actually belong to me. At the same time, the world around me feels completely foggy and artificial, almost like I'm separated from my surroundings by a thick pane of glass.\n\nDoctor: Since Acute Stress Disorder typically lasts up to a month and Posttraumatic Stress Disorder is considered if symptoms last longer than a month, do you think your symptoms might persist beyond the next few weeks, or do you feel they are already improving or will improve soon?\n\nPatient: It has only been about a week since the traumatic event, so everything is still very fresh and overwhelming. Because it is so recent, I'm really not sure whether these symptoms will persist or if they might start to improve soon. I'm just feeling very lost right now and hoping they don't last.\n\nDoctor: Can you tell me more about the traumatic event that triggered these feelings, and how you think it has been affecting you, considering your current symptoms and emotional state?\n\nPatient: To be honest, I can't tell you much about the event itself because my memory of it is so fragmented and mostly blank. All I really recall is that during the event, time felt like it completely stopped or warped in a very strange way. It's incredibly distressing because I'm struggling to piece together what actually happened.\n\nDoctor: \n\nPatient: I'm sorry, I didn't quite catch what you were asking. Could you please clarify what you would like to know next?\n\nDoctor: Are there any specific triggers or cues that you've noticed might help you remember parts of the event, or does it seem like your memory of it is completely inaccessible right now?\n\nPatient: My memory of the event feels mostly blocked and highly fragmented, where I can recall small bits but other parts are completely inaccessible. I haven't noticed any specific triggers or cues that help bring those missing memories back at all. It just feels like a giant blank space that I can't reach.\n\nDoctor: Can you try to estimate how much of the event you can actually remember, and are there any specific details or sensations that you do recall, even if they seem insignificant or unrelated to the rest of the event?\n\nPatient: It's incredibly frustrating and confusing because almost the entire event is a complete blank to me. I can't recall any timeline of what happened, but I vividly remember the sudden, deafening sound of a horn and a flash of bright red light. Aside from those random, isolated sensations, the rest of it is just completely gone."

Subtypes: Impulsive Anger, Verbal Aggression, Physical Aggression, Chronic Irritability\n[S078] Reckless_Self_Destructive_Behavior: Reckless or self-destructive behavior pattern that endangers one's safety or health.
